- **Label beta status honestly** (single-mention): Mark the release as beta and set expectations clearly in the store page or beta label. A barebones release presented as a full game invites backlash. Sources: [Cumin 2026-08 4:43](https://youtu.be/xtuuL-wRFho?t=283). Newest source 2026-08-10.
- **Code along in Studio, then rebuild unaided** (one-creator-repeated): Follow a tutorial with Studio open, then rebuild the same system from scratch without the video. Watching without being in Studio does not transfer skill. Sources: [RoyalDev 2026-08 0:43](https://youtu.be/7cRCq-sJa_Q?t=43), [RoyalDev 2026-08 2:06](https://youtu.be/7cRCq-sJa_Q?t=126). Newest source 2026-08-31.
- **Drill each service in a scratch baseplate** (single-mention): Practise one service at a time (TweenService, Instance.new, RemoteEvents) in a scratch baseplate until you can build it unaided, then combine them into systems. Sources: [RoyalDev 2026-08 0:43](https://youtu.be/7cRCq-sJa_Q?t=43). Newest source 2026-08-31.
- **Budget about two months of practice before shippable scripting** (one-creator-repeated): A beginner practising consistently should expect roughly two months (one creator: October to December) before producing a production-ready game. Plan the first release around that timeline. Sources: [RoyalDev 2026-08 2:46](https://youtu.be/7cRCq-sJa_Q?t=166), [RoyalDev 2026-08 0:01](https://youtu.be/7cRCq-sJa_Q?t=1). Newest source 2026-08-31.
- **Have AI analyse a proven reference game before building** (single-mention): Before building, use AI to break down the systems of a proven reference game and stay close to what already works instead of reinventing it. Sources: [MonarchDev 2026-10 3:51](https://youtu.be/hPWJlFwp5Xs?t=231). Newest source 2026-10-16.
- **Start the first release on one platform** (single-mention): For a first release, pick a single platform and avoid VR or console until you have experience. This narrows testing and build effort. See disagreements for the cross-platform rule. Sources: [MonarchDev 2026-10 7:37](https://youtu.be/hPWJlFwp5Xs?t=457). Newest source 2026-10-16.
- **Budget extra scripting time when building on mobile** (one-creator-repeated): Mobile Studio typing is slow. In one timed build, a mobile developer had about 1 hour left at feature stage and about 4 minutes before publish, so budget extra scripting time and polish on a phone. Sources: [ItsZyonic 2026-10 3:24](https://youtu.be/EulQilcCXAo?t=204), [ItsZyonic 2026-10 4:05](https://youtu.be/EulQilcCXAo?t=245), [ItsZyonic 2026-10 4:05](https://youtu.be/EulQilcCXAo?t=245). Newest source 2026-10-27.
- **In timed builds, pick one mechanic and reserve time to publish** (one-creator-repeated): With a 3-hour limit, keep scope to one simple core mechanic and cut extras. Reserve the final minutes for publishing; the creator published with about 4 minutes to spare. Sources: [ItsZyonic 2026-10 0:00](https://youtu.be/EulQilcCXAo?t=0), [ItsZyonic 2026-10 0:00](https://youtu.be/EulQilcCXAo?t=0), [ItsZyonic 2026-10 4:05](https://youtu.be/EulQilcCXAo?t=245). Newest source 2026-10-27.
- **Reuse scripts across places by changing only the place IDs** (single-mention): When a game spans or clones into another place, duplicate the working scripts and change only the target place IDs. This avoids rebuilding each place from scratch and keeps logic identical across places. Sources: [Suphi Kaner 2022-08 19:12](https://youtu.be/c4DymnlVuL0?t=1152). Newest source 2022-08-09.
- **Keep shared templates in ReplicatedStorage and server-only templates in ServerStorage** (multiple-creators): Store templates that both client and server must read or clone in ReplicatedStorage. Keep per-player templates such as a GUI in ServerStorage and clone them per player, not in Workspace. This keeps the structure organised and updatable. Sources: [B Ricey 2024-01 1:00](https://youtu.be/NDgnMeJ4CbM?t=60), [AlvinBlox 2024-06 2:12](https://youtu.be/PC14bFNer74?t=132). Newest source 2024-06-09.
- **Build player-placed systems in two passes: client first, then server validation** (single-mention): First build client-side behaviour such as snapping and preview so it works. Then add server-side validation, because the server must verify every placement position before it is accepted. Do not ship the client-only pass. Sources: [B Ricey 2024-01 0:40](https://youtu.be/92acKqS_Jo0?t=40). Newest source 2024-01-20.
- **Use Git/GitHub from the first day of a project** (single-mention): Studio undo does not reliably restore changes, and copy-paste collaboration is error-prone. Put the project under version control from the start and commit after each working feature so you can roll back. Sources: [Leif 2025-05 0:26](https://youtu.be/IJDg6tRJmHo?t=26). Newest source 2025-05-19.
- **Scope phone-built games to single-player and script on a computer** (one-creator-repeated): Studio Light lets you build simple games on a phone but has no multiplayer, so keep scope single-player. Typing code on a phone is very slow, so do scripting work on a PC with a real keyboard. Sources: [ItsZyonic 2023-11 0:00](https://youtu.be/IPqBHaZNmNA?t=0), [ItsZyonic 2023-11 2:57](https://youtu.be/IPqBHaZNmNA?t=177). Newest source 2023-11-28.
- **Drop multiplayer from small prototypes** (single-mention): For a small prototype, leave out multiplayer to avoid networking problems and protect the time budget. Add networking only after the core loop shows it is fun and worth building. Sources: [BloxxedDev 2025-03 1:09](https://youtu.be/CipKM4DBWxg?t=69). Newest source 2025-03-09.
- **Switch to manual scripting when AI fix loops stall** (single-mention): Use AI to draft scaffolding, but expect heavy debugging. If repeated prompts keep producing fixes that break, stop and write the code manually; one creator judged manual scripting faster. Current AI reliability may be better than this 2025 view. Sources: [ByteBlox 2025-01 24:50](https://youtu.be/SCtYVCRVzM8?t=1490), [ByteBlox 2025-01 24:50](https://youtu.be/SCtYVCRVzM8?t=1490). Newest source 2025-01-24.
- **Use the rename-symbol refactor instead of find and replace** (single-mention): When a variable or symbol changes name, use Studio's rename-symbol refactoring to rename it across the script. Manual find and replace is slower and can miss or over-rename references. Sources: [SimTek Game Development 2025-09 28:19](https://youtu.be/EQqd8Xk3Wkk?t=1699). Newest source 2025-09-27.
- **Publish the experience before creating gamepasses** (single-mention): Gamepasses can only be created for a published experience, so publish the place first, then create the passes. The source is from 2020, so confirm the current Creator Hub flow before relying on it. Sources: [TheDevKing 2020-03 1:53](https://youtu.be/RmWgJVjJrqE?t=113). Newest source 2020-03-12.
- **Break the game into selling points before ideating** (single-mention): Start ideation by listing the game's selling points, what the player can expect to do, and what the game should do for the player. Feed this list into the game design doc and the core player loop. Sources: [GameMakers 2022-03 16:23](https://youtu.be/t-jpyaQffYE?t=983). Newest source 2022-03-01.
- **Verify each release is measurably better than the last** (single-mention): The speaker says most creators believe they improve but do not. Compare concrete metrics such as retention, playtime and CCU between releases before calling an update an improvement. Sources: [SmartyRBX 2024-11 30:50](https://youtu.be/Pwy9U0B8tJk?t=1850). Newest source 2024-11-04.
- **Add content until progression feels balanced** (one-creator-repeated): One creator found five backpacks insufficient to balance progression and added three more (eight total). Treat content volume as a balancing lever and confirm the result with playtests. See the disagreement with the existing 'verify fun before adding content' rule. Sources: [janisjanis01 2025-01 14:53](https://youtu.be/EpPU3Am8lgE?t=893), [janisjanis01 2025-01 14:53](https://youtu.be/EpPU3Am8lgE?t=893). Newest source 2025-01-05.
- **Learn scripting first when the goal is a full game** (one-creator-repeated): The speaker says scripting is about 90% of a game and the biggest learning curve, so prioritise it over other disciplines. Pair this with daily Studio practice on buttons and simple maps. Sources: [VuukStudios 2026-03 1:05](https://youtu.be/ba1raV5HE-c?t=65), [VuukStudios 2026-03 16:06](https://youtu.be/ba1raV5HE-c?t=966). Newest source 2026-03-18.
- **Combine skills into bigger games once small projects work** (single-mention): After small single-skill projects work, combine skills into a larger game. Scripting in particular can lead to a full game. This is the step after focused repetition on one skill. Sources: [SmartyRBX 2024-11 1:57](https://youtu.be/Pwy9U0B8tJk?t=117). Newest source 2024-11-04.
- **Confirm traction before raising money or spending on user acquisition** (single-mention): Before soft launch, maximise the learning you get per dollar spent. Do not raise more funding or spend on UA until the game shows traction. Sources: [GameMakers 2025-02 9:45](https://youtu.be/qZRNpN5M8jA?t=585). Newest source 2025-02-04.
- **Batch-build content to hit a close deadline** (one-creator-repeated): Split the content into fixed batches and build one batch at a time. janisjanis01 speedran three new spring areas with one day left before an update, added alongside new pets and eggs. His hoverboard work was done in batches of four designs. Self-reported; treat batch size as a planning tool, not a fixed rule. Sources: [janisjanis01 2024-06 10:18](https://youtu.be/JeyYzEjPjc8?t=618), [janisjanis01 2024-06 9:36](https://youtu.be/JeyYzEjPjc8?t=576), [janisjanis01 2024-06 10:18](https://youtu.be/JeyYzEjPjc8?t=618), [janisjanis01 2024-11 2:46](https://youtu.be/yxd5GMEjtDA?t=166). Newest source 2024-11-03.
- **Package the game once the core idea is strong** (one-creator-repeated): rasdev: do not keep making small title, icon, and description tweaks on a weak idea. First make the core loop work. Once it does, build the title and icon before finishing the game so the game is ready to show. Sources: [rasdev 2026-05 1:21](https://youtu.be/wGRgITzP18c?t=81), [rasdev 2026-05 4:04](https://youtu.be/wGRgITzP18c?t=244). Newest source 2026-05-07.
- **Cut steps that add friction without adding fun** (single-mention): janisjanis01 removed a carry-crops-to-base mechanic and switched to direct inventory collection to keep the loop simpler. Test each extra step in the core loop and remove it if it only adds travel or chores. Sources: [janisjanis01 2025-09 4:26](https://youtu.be/uNLDi_GO2MA?t=266). Newest source 2025-09-21.
- **Film build progress for social content** (single-mention): janisjanis01 uses build montages from speedruns as social content. Use the footage you already capture during the build rather than staging separate recording time. Sources: [janisjanis01 2024-06 10:18](https://youtu.be/JeyYzEjPjc8?t=618). Newest source 2024-06-30.
- **Experiment with visuals until they look right** (single-mention): janisjanis01: try variations of a look until one works rather than planning every visual detail up front. Keep this inside a time-box so experimentation does not eat the schedule. Sources: [janisjanis01 2024-03 2:56](https://youtu.be/9gBS7nhaC3c?t=176). Newest source 2024-03-29.
- **Spread transparent, non-collidable spawn locations around the lobby** (single-mention): Build a simple lobby first and place transparent, non-collidable SpawnLocation parts in several spots around it, so players spawn in varied places instead of stacking on one point. Keep the lobby simple at this stage. Sources: [AlvinBlox 2018-12 1:00](https://youtu.be/vCpl5M_9mcQ?t=60). Newest source 2018-12-08.

## Checklist
- [ ] Define one core loop and cut everything else for the MVP
- [ ] Write a light GDD (concept, features, monetisation, controls, UI, roles)
- [ ] Convert the GDD into per-discipline Trello/Jira lists with owners and deadlines
- [ ] Paper-prototype UI/rules; Studio-prototype risky mechanics
- [ ] Graybox and playtest the map before final art
- [ ] Put all text in localization tables from day one
- [ ] Build map and UI before scripting systems
- [ ] Create a reusable game template
- [ ] Set milestones with buffer (solo ~2x estimates; 6 productive hours per day)
- [ ] Check performance continuously
- [ ] Soft-launch with small ad spend; watch players for 7-14 days
- [ ] Judge viability in the first 1-2 weeks; shelve games with no traction
- [ ] Keep a content backlog and a 3-month rolling roadmap
- [ ] Limit cadence releases to 2-3 weeks with no new systems
- [ ] Gate risky updates behind config flags and roll out gradually
- [ ] Use Git and staging; test before pushing updates
- [ ] Run a retro after every update and track KPIs
- [ ] Triage feedback by severity, breadth and cost
- [ ] After each update, check the target metric and revert if not improved
- [ ] Keep updates to 2-3 goals
- [ ] Use AI step by step with playtests between prompts
- [ ] Keep backup scripters and clear specs for hires
- [ ] Schedule sustainable hours; cancel events that aren't ready
- [ ] Spend ~80% on game development, ~20% on marketing
- [ ] Avoid abandoning live games that still have an audience
- [ ] Start in under an hour: sign up, play a few games, install Studio, take the onboarding tour, then build the 3D platformer tutorial.
- [ ] Start with free Studio and built-in services; no upfront tooling cost is needed.
- [ ] Publish a place to the cloud early so the data model is saved and you can work from any computer.
- [ ] Build the minimal MVP around one core loop, then plan a bite-size 5-6 week release.
- [ ] Write asset and tech requests before each content cadence; split evergreen and seasonal assets.
- [ ] Stage config changes in Studio, and use SetTestingValue to test on one server before going live.
- [ ] Create events as private and switch to public only when you promote them.
- [ ] Check badge count against the 5-free-per-24h GMT limit before creating badges; batch creation.
- [ ] Set the source language before starting localization, then add supported languages.
- [ ] Leave Localization auto-capture on so game text lands in a translation table early.
- [ ] Use only assets you own or are licensed to use.
- [ ] Re-take the questionnaire and resubmit whenever an update changes an answer.
- [ ] Check the Creator Dashboard banner daily for reduced-exposure status; file a Bug Report with Universe ID if it looks wrong.
- [ ] Use the Create alert shortcut from a Performance chart's overflow menu to pre-fill metric and filters.
- [ ] Verify a memory-leak fix by checking the next update's crash chart.
- [ ] Build performance best practices in from the start, not after publishing.
- [ ] Hold a daily (minimum twice-weekly) team review of completed and planned tasks.
- [ ] Set incremental goals when learning Lua, and dissect other people's game code line by line.
- [ ] Sync scripts with Studio file sync in VS Code instead of adding Rojo.
- [ ] Start with paper prototypes for early exploration, then prototype in Studio once ideas are settled.
- [ ] Hold monetisation spend until you reach roughly 10 DAU; focus on D1 retention and playtime first.
- [ ] Build reusable quest, collection and event systems to cut future update time.
- [ ] Decide the access model before building paid content: public game, no private servers, copying disabled.
- [ ] Define milestones (prototype, vertical slice, MVP) and record which one you are at.
- [ ] Check team size and weekly hours against the genre before committing to it.
- [ ] For a solo beginner, scope the first release to a 2-4 week MVP at 5-15 hours per week.
- [ ] Count content items times variants (e.g. designs x variants) before starting art.
- [ ] Mark any feature likely to exceed 2-3 weeks and plan a cheap stand-in for it.
- [ ] Build a playable within the first few days using Parts and Creator Store placeholders.
- [ ] Set a time limit per build step and reserve at least 5 minutes for testing.
- [ ] Publish every ten minutes or after any big change; use Alt+P (Option+P on Mac).
- [ ] Duplicate the place or project before risky refactors; keep backups.
- [ ] Test each mechanic with a print or a Play run before building on top of it.
- [ ] Verify RNG/luck outcomes and server-lock expiry before launch.
- [ ] Put tunable values in one GameSettings module with rough starting values, then tune in playtests.
- [ ] Keep a template place with standard structure, modules, configs and services for new projects.
- [ ] Keep a shared to-do list anyone can pick up tasks from.
- [ ] Write a hand-off list when passing work between developers and cross-review code.
- [ ] Finish one phase (layout, test map, code, polish) before starting the next.
- [ ] Playtest after each small step, not only at the end.
- [ ] Ask AI to simplify scope when the plan is too large; add features one prompt at a time.
- [ ] Review AI-generated code and fall back to official docs when stuck.
- [ ] Finish the official first-game or Baseplate tutorial before building custom systems.
- [ ] Publish the game before inviting friends or playtesters.
- [ ] Allow up to 24 hours of moderation before marketplace items can be enabled for sale.
- [ ] Remember a public app cannot go back to private; plan resubmission for later edits.
- [ ] Judge ad experiments at day 5 by benchmarks and the core loop.
- [ ] Budget rework time when integrating a third-party framework into an existing game.
- [ ] Break each feature into numbered steps before building it, and test each step in isolation.
- [ ] Write a user story for each feature in Who / When / Why form before assigning it.
- [ ] Document asset specs (hit parts, poly count, style) before modelers start, so output drops into code without rework.
- [ ] Set up a board with columns for concept, systems, monetisation, building/art and paid costs for each game.
- [ ] Plan 2-3 week sprints; at sprint end move unfinished items out instead of extending the sprint.
- [ ] Work at least 30-60 minutes a day on the project, not long sessions a few times a week.
- [ ] Use 15-minute focused work blocks with 5-minute breaks.
- [ ] Never let a project sit untouched for more than two days in a row.
- [ ] Before showing testers, check fun, challenge and error-free; keep pre-feedback fixes to small single changes.
- [ ] Give each playtest a one-minute goal and a specific task to check.
- [ ] Plan a 7-30 day beta and track D1 and D30 retention before the wider release.
- [ ] Run projected Robux earnings through the DevEx calculator before starting a long monetised build.
- [ ] Tidy the workspace into named folders at the end of every session.
- [ ] Make one small change at a time and undo the last one if the project breaks.
- [ ] Enable Team Create, or recover lost work from File > Advanced > Open Autosaves.
- [ ] Customise shortcuts (File > Customize Shortcuts) and use the Studio Settings search (Alt+S / Option+S).
- [ ] Keep Studio updated; outdated versions lack the newest APIs and features.
- [ ] Check the Early Access opt-in in account settings before editing a live game or Team Create place.
- [ ] Enable AvatarJointUpgrade or other upcoming betas in a test place early to see breaking changes.
- [ ] Report API gaps to the DevForum with a use case and a repro.
- [ ] Limit world-building for a first build to about a week (e.g. one house with a lawn), and expand later.
- [ ] Use one main AI session for integration; don't run several sessions editing the same code.
- [ ] Before a build, remove the baseplate and build only the core action to test whether the idea is worth pursuing.
- [ ] Log every new idea the same day in a shared list or board.
- [ ] Write a one-page design doc: name, reference games, genre, core loop, progression, onboarding, monetisation.
- [ ] List every script system (map generation, ragdoll, UI) and every build, UI, VFX and model need so remaining work is visible.
- [ ] Map the full loop, including wrong-input handling, cleanup and reset, before coding.
- [ ] Split the design doc into tabs or sections per area (systems, builds, animations, attributes).
- [ ] Put each task on a board with a checklist, and set a fixed deadline on each card with notifications on.
- [ ] Before a big update, design data structures for all systems up front.
- [ ] Budget for slips on big updates: plan a 2-month update as possibly 6-7 months.
- [ ] Reuse working systems from older projects for the first barebones prototype.
- [ ] Test risky systems in a small standalone place before building the full map.
- [ ] Enable Team Create per place and keep Collaborative Editing on.
- [ ] Commit script drafts promptly and resolve conflicts with Merge From Server.
- [ ] Install UI Labs to iterate on UI without launching the game.
- [ ] Group UI components into a storybook ModuleScript with storyRoots set to script.
- [ ] Outsource a core system after your own attempt keeps failing.
- [ ] After a rough playtest, reserve about two weeks for bugs plus console and mobile support before wider launch.
- [ ] Cut mechanics that testing shows hurt controls or tempo.
- [ ] Keep a live game running unless it is game-breaking.
- [ ] Read and understand AI-generated code before shipping it.
- [ ] Enter a game jam or hack week to test a concept's fun quickly.
- [ ] Build a monetised side project to show in job applications.
- [ ] Add a post-launch update budget (time and cost) to the full-cycle plan before launch.
- [ ] Before depending on a Roblox API labelled alpha, test its behaviour in Studio and plan for bugs and changes.
- [ ] Keep a working prototype small and presentable so it can be shown to a potential partner or collaborator.
- [ ] Before a significant feature, run an AI interrogation session (around 10 minutes, about 13 questions) and record each answer as a decision.
- [ ] Write the feature's plan with AI in plan mode, then read the generated plan markdown and fix it before the agent builds.
- [ ] Store Open Cloud keys on disk, not in chat, and set standing instructions for the agent.
- [ ] Turn on auto-publish from commits so each change can be tested on a phone within about a minute.
- [ ] Run Scene Analysis during each build session, not just before launch.
- [ ] Commit reusable agent skills to the project repo for teammates to reuse.
- [ ] Give interactive props their functional parts (axles, motors, scripts) in the scope estimate.
- [ ] Track your roadmap on-time shipping rate; Roblox's reported benchmark is 79%.
- [ ] If a live game's base is outgrown, plan a foundation rebuild rather than a full game replacement.
- [ ] Playtest your build during the work session, not only at scheduled reviews.
- [ ] Shorten growth, cooldown and timer values in a test config so the full loop can be verified in minutes; restore them before release.
- [ ] Label any early release as beta on the store page and state what is missing.
- [ ] Follow tutorials with Studio open, then rebuild the same system without the video.
- [ ] Practise each service (TweenService, Instance.new, RemoteEvents) alone in a scratch baseplate until you can build it unaided.
- [ ] Plan about two months of daily practice before expecting a shippable first game.
- [ ] Ask AI to break down a proven reference game's systems before you start building.
- [ ] Choose one platform for the first release; defer VR and console until you have experience.
- [ ] On mobile, add extra time for scripting and typing in Studio.
- [ ] In a timed build, limit scope to one core mechanic.
- [ ] Reserve the final minutes of a timed build for publishing.
- [ ] Track the share of roadmap or sprint items shipped on time and compare to the 79% platform benchmark.
- [ ] Initialise version control (Git/GitHub) before the first real Studio session, and commit after each working feature.
- [ ] When creating a new place from an existing game, duplicate the working scripts and change only the target place IDs.
- [ ] Store shared templates in ReplicatedStorage and per-player GUI templates in ServerStorage; clone them at runtime.
- [ ] For a placement or build-by-player feature, get the client snapping working first, then add server validation before release.
- [ ] For a phone-built game, plan a single-player scope from the start.
- [ ] Do scripting work on a computer with a real keyboard, not a phone.
- [ ] For a small prototype, exclude multiplayer and add networking after the core loop is proven.
- [ ] If AI-generated fixes keep failing after a few tries, stop prompting and write the code by hand.
- [ ] Use the rename-symbol refactor in Studio when renaming a variable or symbol.
- [ ] If a tutorial's model or file does not match your Studio version, use the version linked in its description (T17996 is from 2021; check it still matches the current Studio).
- [ ] Publish the place before creating any gamepass.
- [ ] Write the game's selling points and what the player and game do for each before drafting the design doc.
- [ ] Compare retention, playtime and CCU between releases to confirm each update improved the game.
- [ ] After playtests show unbalanced progression, add content (e.g. more items) and retest rather than only tuning numbers.
- [ ] Practise in Studio for 2-3 hours a day on buttons and simple maps, more if possible.
- [ ] Once small single-skill projects work, combine skills into a bigger game.
- [ ] Confirm traction before raising money or spending on UA; keep soft-launch spend focused on learning.
- [ ] Before a deadline, list content items and group them into fixed batches; build one batch at a time and drop the batch that will not fit.
- [ ] Compare the planned count of icons, models, or areas against your usual per-update output before committing to scope.
- [ ] Confirm the core loop is fun in a playtest before spending time on title, icon, or description.
- [ ] Once the core idea works, make the title and icon before finishing the game so it is ready to show.
- [ ] Review each step in the core loop and remove any that adds travel or chores without adding fun.
- [ ] Record build progress during work so you have montage footage for social posts without a separate filming session.
- [ ] When setting roadmap dates, plan for roughly 21% of items to slip past their target (79% on-time base rate).
- [ ] Log total build hours per project so future scope estimates use real figures.
- [ ] Build a simple lobby before the main game, and place several transparent, non-collidable spawn locations around it so players do not stack on one point.
- [ ] When planning roadmap dates, budget for a realistic miss rate; a platform-wide benchmark of about 79% on-time shipping (about 1 in 5 items late) is a reasonable starting assumption.

| Time to rebuild a stalled first game | 5 days | Self-reported 2024-10: a fresh rebuild with a new design, instead of updating the dead game. | [janisjanis01 2024-10 2:09](https://youtu.be/ZyQ73kfh_b4?t=129) |
| On-time roadmap shipping rate | 79% | Share of roadmap items Roblox shipped on time since the last Roblox Developer Conference (RDC), reported 2026-09. Official platform figure; implies roughly 21% of items slipped. Use as a planning reference for miss rates, not as an indie benchmark. | [Roblox DevForum 2026-09](https://devforum.roblox.com/t/creator-roadmap-2026-fall-update/4880208) |

## Where creators agree
- Ship a barebones MVP and iterate on live feedback (SmartyRBX, AlvinBlox, Tizzy, MoseyTube, Roblox Creators, Roblox).
- Start with small simple games, not a dream game (SmartyRBX, AlvinBlox, Zeal/Creators on the Blox, Roblox Creators).
- Prototype cheaply before full builds (Roblox Creators officials, Roblox).
- Use design docs and Trello/Jira task boards (SmartyRBX, Tizzy, Roblox Creators).
- Keep cadence content small and built on existing systems (Roblox Creators, Jasper/Creators on the Blox).
- Avoid over-engineering and crunch (Roblox Creators, Creators on the Blox).
- Roblox's fast deploy cycle favors rapid iteration (Roblox, SmartyRBX, Daiorg, RobloxCreators).
- Build and ship a small MVP quickly, then iterate on player feedback (T4805, T3784, T4067, T3355).
- Roblox handles netcode and backend, so creators should focus on gameplay (T3365, T4335, Q809).
- Publishing is fast and cheap, and there is no approval queue for updates (T3364, T4337, Q810).
- Expect early failures and learn from them rather than quitting after one flop (T3514, N1549, T3554).
- Prototype before full build and keep prototypes narrow (T3874, T3877, T3876).
- Keep the first release small and aim for completion over perfection (Q694, T3459, T4805).
- Ship a minimal playable early and iterate on player feedback (Roblox Creators, rasdev, Yann, Duckable).
- Keep scope small; big ambitious projects burn out motivation (Duckable, rasdev, Roblox Creators).
- Developers consistently underestimate how long tasks take (Yann; Duckable's honeymoon-slowdown account).
- Publish or save frequently to avoid losing work (Roblox Creator Docs across many items).
- Speed and shipping matter more than polish or bug-free code early on (rasdev, B Ricey, Crusherfire).
- Prototype the core loop first and add placeholders before art (Roblox Creators, Yann, rasdev).
- Start with a small finishable project rather than a dream game (Roblox Creators, 1Coal, janisjanis01, Duckable).
- Ship small, finishable games before ambitious ones; a finished small game teaches more than an unfinished large one (T13405, T13019, Q1374, T11190).
- Release the playable core loop as a base version and iterate with updates instead of polishing for months (T13631, T13746, Q1399, Q1344).
- Keep shipping updates after release; work on the game regularly so it doesn't go idle (T13666, T13743, Q1422).
- Consistent, short, daily or frequent work beats occasional marathon sessions (T13635, Q1401, T10937).
- Plan scope before building: agree on features, standards and handoffs (T10683, T12459, T12722).
- Keep saves frequent: publish every ten minutes or after a big change (T14545, T15049, N5747).
- Plan the design and loop on paper before building (RoyalDev, LovJak, Lnor Roblox).
- Reuse existing systems, templates and parts to save build time (Last Level Studios, Emancyphur, Mulch, LovJak).
- Do not rush a release; polish before wider launch or paid traffic (Last Level Studios, Mulch, studio rook).
- Final stretches and big updates run longer than estimated (Lnor Roblox, Last Level Studios).
- Plan before building: plan mode (T17719) and AI grilling (T17709) reinforce the existing rules to write numbered steps and plan the full loop before building.
- Fast iteration with quick feedback: auto-publish on commit (T17713) supports the existing rule to iterate many times a day without review waits.
- Build from known-good parts: shared agent skills (T17711) reinforce reusing existing parts and templates.
- Cumin (T17813) and Deconstructor of Fun (T17957, T17958) both warn against over-scoped architecture or scope and favour reaching a playable, finishable loop first.
- Reusing templates and existing structure saves time: the existing templates rule is supported by B Ricey (T18074), AlvinBlox (T18129) and Suphi Kaner (T18034).
- Small builds should cut scope to the core mechanic and drop extras: BloxxedDev (T18210, T18211) and ItsZyonic (T18070) both limit scope to protect time, and the existing time-box and scope-cut rules agree.
- Use version control and not undo alone: Leif (T18242) supports the existing safe-release and version control rule.

## Where creators disagree
- **Ship rough vs delay for quality**
  - AlvinBlox, SmartyRBX, Roblox Creators (T225, T255): ship MVP, done is better than perfect
  - Roblox Creators (T214, T267): push the date when testers say it isn't ready; first impression matters
  - Creators on the Blox (T2373): only ships games meeting a higher bar
- **Detail of planning**
  - Roblox Creators (T252, N205): write every detail up front (two months of GDD)
  - SmartyRBX (T1334), Daiorg (T2349): light/flexible GDD, plan only ~20 minutes of play
  - Doors devs (T2464): poor upfront planning hurt Floor 2
- **Roadmap horizon**
  - Roblox Creators 2021 (T357, T448): at least three months out, plan two years of vision
  - Roblox 2024 (T1461): quarter-to-6-month chunks, not a full year (newer; prefer)
- **Abandoning games**
  - Roblox Creators (T518), waveshine, Tizzy: ditch quickly if no traction
  - kra_DeZ (T2982): don't abandon a live game; Creators on the Blox: hype games are quick money, move on (T3309)
- **Importance of passion/long projects**
  - SmartyRBX (T1683): results over passion
  - kra_DeZ (T3115): time isn't income
  - Creators on the Blox/Jailbreak, Doors: long passion projects succeeded (N1100, N979)
- **Free models/asset reuse**
  - Roblox Creators (T572): fine, no complaints
  - CallMeDogey (Q313): free-model game being reworked
- **Tooling**
  - Roblox (T2050): minimize external IDEs, use native Studio
  - Leif (T1787, T1790): use Git and Wally
- **When to monetise**
  - Existing knowledge file rule: monetise early and design monetisation in (no source ID given).
  - DevForum creator (T3450, 2022): do not focus on monetisation below ~10 DAU; fix D1 retention and playtime first.
- **How polished the first release should be**
  - DevForum creator (T3459, 2022): aim for a finished, polished simple core loop first.
  - Existing knowledge file rule and Roblox docs (T4805): build a barebones MVP, then iterate.
- **Responding to community feedback and scope**
  - DitchyDevelopments (T3721, 2025): adding community-requested obby and race modes expanded scope fast; he later said it probably wasn't a good idea.
  - Deconstructor of Fun (T3784, 2026): live-op weekly with the community based on player feedback.
- **Whether weekly live ops are required**
  - Deconstructor of Fun (T3784, 2026): ship then live-op weekly with the community.
  - Deconstructor of Fun (N1715, 2026): a SpongeBob hyper-casual game reached ~100M visits with no weekly live ops, updated only once or twice.
- **How much AI should write in one go**
  - Roblox official demo (Q719, 2024): the assistant did 100% of the scripts in that demo.
  - Existing knowledge file rule: use AI tools step by step, not in one prompt.
- **Time needed for a solo MVP**
  - Existing knowledge file rule: ship a bite-size MVP in 5-6 weeks.
  - rasdev (T6562, T6588, 2026-06): 2-4 weeks for a solo beginner at 5-15 hours per week. Treat this as the newer figure and prefer it.
  - Yann (N2843, 2026-07): a solo simulator in about 3 weeks was feasible.
- **Planning versus shipping speed**
  - rasdev (Q1106, 2026-10): 90% of success comes from planning done beforehand.
  - rasdev (T6654, 2026-07): spend more time refining the idea before building.
  - rasdev (T6648, Q1059, 2026-07): don't wait for the perfect game or moment; ship the MVP.
  - rasdev (T6881, 2026-10): plan thoroughly, then ship quickly.
- **Refactoring and rework cost**
  - B Ricey (T5745, 2022-09): refactor freely; it costs less than guessing the perfect structure.
  - RoDev (T6195, 2025-05): unplanned changes to an existing codebase are costly.
  - janisjanis01 (T6316, 2025-09): budget time for a full script rewrite when adding a third-party framework.
- **When and how to use AI**
  - Duckable (T6765, 2026-09): beginners should learn without AI first.
  - Duckable (T6464, 2026-05): use AI for mundane coding but review output; error-prone for inventory.
  - Duckable (T6553, 2026-05): use official docs when stuck rather than AI generation.
  - Crusherfire (T6905, 2026-10): use AI to prototype several variants quickly.
  - tef (T6887, 2026-10): build in iterative AI passes with a numbered fix list.
- **Prototype duration**
  - Roblox Creators (T5705, 2022-06): prototype the first 2-3 weeks before building maps, art and features.
  - Roblox Creator Docs (T9936, 2026-10): playtest within the first few days using placeholders.
  - These can be reconciled: the first is the full core-loop prototype; the second is a rough playable.
- **Speed versus patience**
  - VuukStudios (T13484, N5404) and Raul (N5417) say a simple trend game can be shipped in 4-5 days or a full game in 4 days.
  - VuukStudios (T13944) says quick trend or template releases have high variance and short lives, while a core idea built brick by brick lasts for years.
  - Roblox DevForum authors (T10437, T10484, Q1131) say don't rush; growth takes over a year and a trend alone doesn't save a messy project.
  - Erthlord (T12971, Q1340) says a 7-day trend game was too tight and needed about 9 days.
- **Build duration target**
  - VuukStudios (T13833, N5456, 2026-09) says 1-3 weeks, 4 weeks at most.
  - VuukStudios (T13019, N5021, 2025-11) says 2-4 weeks for early games.
  - The existing knowledge file rule suggests a bite-size MVP in 5-6 weeks; the newer 2026 guidance is shorter and should be preferred for solo beginners.
- **AI for game code**
  - Matt The Method (T13737, N5041) says AI handles repetitive work and built two games with it, though both flopped partly from stopped updates (T13743).
  - VuukStudios (T13834) says AI-only code gets buggy, unreadable and crashes servers, so learn scripting first.
- **Version control and file sync tooling**
  - DevForum posts from 2020-2022 recommend Rojo with Git and an external editor (T10853, T11240, T11380, T11372).
  - Newer guidance in the existing rules favours native file sync instead of Rojo; migrating a far-along project is costly (T10858).
- **Using AI for logic and systems**
  - RoyalDev (T16631, N6647): use AI for logic and systems to cut build time dramatically.
  - Existing knowledge rule (prior sources): use AI for repetitive work, not for decisions or parallel code edits. Reconcile by reading and understanding all AI code (T16622) before use.
- **What to do with an underperforming or aging game**
  - Last Level Studios (T15905, Q1495): keep a live game running unless it is game-breaking; launch a new game rather than replace a performing one.
  - RoyalDev (T16604) and Mulch (Q1527): rebuild or overhaul an underperforming game rather than abandon it.
  - Reconcile: keep running while it earns, rebuild when plays decline, replace only if broken.
- **Can AI take part in design decisions?**
  - Existing rule: use AI for repetitive work, not for decisions.
  - Roblox official (T17709, N7574): have AI interrogate you before a significant feature. It asks questions and surfaces gaps, while the human makes the 13 decisions. Reconciliation: AI as questioner is compatible with the rule; AI as decider is not.
- **Platform scope for the first release**
  - Existing file rule: 'Publish once for all platforms' (cross-platform from the start).
  - MonarchDev (T17931, 2026-10): start on one platform and avoid VR or console until you have experience.
  - Reconciliation: the newer MonarchDev advice applies to a first release by an inexperienced developer; cross-platform publishing still makes sense once the team is experienced.
- **Multiplayer in early builds**
  - Existing rule: 'Lean on built-in multiplayer and backend'.
  - BloxxedDev (T18211): drop multiplayer for a small prototype to skip networking problems.
  - ItsZyonic (T18070): phone-built Studio Light games have no multiplayer, so plan single-player scope.
- **Using AI for scripts**
  - Existing rule: 'Use AI assistants for scripts and mundane tasks'.
  - ByteBlox (T18165, 2025-01): AI scaffolding needs heavy debugging, and manual scripting was faster than repeated fix prompts.
  - ByteBlox (Q1852, 2025-01): AI is not yet flawless, so human scripting is still needed.
- **Whether to add content or verify fun first when progression feels unbalanced**
  - janisjanis01 (T18560, Q1899): keep adding content (three more backpacks) until progression feels balanced.
  - Existing KB rule (no named creator): 'Verify fun before adding content'. Prefer this rule when the core loop is not yet fun; use janisjanis01's approach only after fun is confirmed.
- **Which skill to learn first**
  - VuukStudios (T18637): learn scripting first; it is about 90% of a game.
  - Existing KB rule (no named creator): 'Try each discipline briefly before specialising'. Reconcile by trying other disciplines briefly, then prioritising scripting for a full game.
- **When to finish title and icon relative to the game**
  - rasdev (T18944): do not keep tweaking title, icon, and description on a weak idea; the core idea must be strong first.
  - rasdev (T18945): package the game (title, icon) before finishing it.
  - Reconciled: both hold when read together. Packaging is worth doing once the core loop is proven; before that it is wasted polish.

## Date context (what may be outdated)
- Much 2021-2022 official talk was by large-game devs; 2024+ advice favors small scope and fast MVPs.
- Roadmap horizon advice changed from 3+ months (2021) to quarter-to-6-month chunks (2024).
- 2018 melee and localization advice is old; machine translation was available by 2021-12.
- Studio speed, expedited review price (50,000 Robux, cut from 100,000) and deploy times are 2025-2026 figures and may change.
- AI-assisted building (Claude MCP, 2026) is recent; Q566 claims ability has improved.
- Short-form content outperforming long-form is a 2025 claim (T2320).
- Roblox Creator Docs entries are dated 2026-10, the most recent source set. Prefer them for current platform rules, such as the 5-free-badges-per-24h limit and no approval queue. Verify before relying on them, since they may change.
- GameMakers advice (2020-03) predates current publishing, moderation and the free-badge limit. Treat its tooling advice as historical.
- DevForum timing and monetisation advice (2022-2023) is older than the 2026 creator guidance; prefer the 2026 bite-size MVP timeline (5-6 weeks) over the older 3-4 month estimate.
- Open Cloud testing and Luau CI (T3618, 2024-09) is described as future (once it lands). Check current availability before recommending it.
- Studio file sync (T3617, 2024-09) is the current native option; it supersedes third-party Rojo workflows for many creators.
- AI productivity figures (N1601, N1602, 2024-09) are self-reported or speaker-cited estimates, not controlled measurements.
- Creator figures about flops, DAU thresholds and bite-size timelines are self-reported and may not generalise.
- 2026 guidance (rasdev, 2026-05 to 2026-10) gives a solo MVP target of 2-4 weeks. This supersedes the existing 5-6 week rule for beginners; keep the older figure only for teams.
- Roblox Creator Docs entries dated 2026-10 include many classroom and education lesson timings (lesson durations, pose limits, student time windows). Do not generalise these to commercial game development.
- Attributes were in beta in January 2021 (T5384). Beta flags may now be stable; check current Studio before following old beta instructions.
- The Character Controller Library (T7347, T9120) is opt-in beta as of 2026-10; expect changes.
- Moderation of up to 24 hours (T7097, 2026-10) applies to current marketplace uploads.
- AI tool advice is volatile. Items from 2025-08 onward may not reflect current tool behaviour; prefer the latest dated source and re-verify.
- Script Sync and InExperienceConfig limits (N3410, N3506, N3507) are current as of 2026-10 and may change.
- Duckable's 2025 timelines (Fisch, about 4 months) and 2026 Duckable advice reflect different scales; check the project scope before comparing.
- ProfileService is stable but no longer supported; use ProfileStore for new projects (T10714, 2020-07).
- Rojo plus Git and VSCode were the standard workflow in 2020-2022 (T10853, T11240, T11380); later guidance favours native file sync, so treat those posts as older advice.
- TextChatService is the default for new projects since 2024; switch to Legacy only for old code (T12051, 2024-03).
- Server Authority was in beta from 2025-12 with a general availability target of early 2026 (N5045, T13080, T13090). Check the current status before relying on the setup steps, since they may have changed.
- Avatar Abilities beta and the AvatarJointUpgrade flag (Phase 1 default Disabled, no breaking change) were announced in 2026-01 and 2026-02 (T13160, T13196). Check current status.
- Breaking API changes during beta are limited to once a week, announced a day ahead (T13207, 2026-02).
- The Early Access opt-in in account settings can make live edits unplayable for non-program users; revert before editing (T13466, 2026-06).
- Roblox Studio recommended specs are Windows 11 or macOS 14+ (N5985), with minimum Windows 10 or macOS 10.14 (N5984). Official docs updated 2026-10.
- Creator-recorded timings (2016-2021 DevForum, 2024 GnomeCode tutorials) predate several engine changes; treat them as process guidance, not current API advice.
- The existing rule on a 5-6 week bite-size MVP conflicts with newer 2026 guidance of 1-3 weeks; prefer the newer figure for solo work.
- Sources span 2016-2026. The newest items (2026: RoyalDev, studio rook, LovJak) show AI-assisted builds of simple games in under a week, which is faster than the 2019-2022 Last Level Studios timelines (months per project). Treat the AI-speed figures as recent and self-reported.
- Roblox tooling advice (Team Create, Collaborative Editing, Iris, UI Labs, Wally) is version-dependent. Team Create guidance dates to 2021 and UI Labs/Iris to 2025; verify menu paths and package versions against current Roblox documentation.
- Last Level Studios' figures (2019-2023) describe older workflows and large codebases. Use them for patterns, not exact timelines.
- Lnor Roblox's 2025-12 timelines show the largest recent schedule overrun (planned 2 months, took about 7).
- 2026-11: The official Roblox public API is flagged as alpha (Q1763): expect bugs and changes. This supports the existing 'Expect breaking changes while a feature is in beta' rule and applies it to alpha APIs. The quote does not name which API, so verify the specific API's status.
- 2026-08: Agency guidance (T17517) recommends budgeting post-launch updates as a permanent line item. This is newer than the existing 'Ship frequent updates' advice and does not conflict with it. It is a single agency source with self-reported data.
- No conflicts with existing rules were found; the new items extend or narrow earlier advice rather than replacing it.
- As of 2026-11, Roblox's Compute functions (serverless Luau for event-driven or scheduled work, T17638) are announced for Early 2027. Don't plan around them as available yet.
- The October 2026 workflow items (plan mode, Open Cloud auto-publish, shared agent skills, Scene Analysis during builds) are the newest advice. Prefer them over older manual-publish assumptions where they conflict.
- The RDC figures (N7486, N7551) are from 2026-09 and cover the period since the previous RDC. They are not a fixed annual benchmark.
- Existing rule 'Use native file sync instead of Rojo' and the save-every-ten-minutes rule still apply; auto-publish supplements them rather than replacing them.
- The 79% on-time figure is dated 2026-09. Treat it as a point-in-time platform benchmark and check for newer RDC figures before relying on it.
- N7593 is dated 2026-09 and comes from an official Roblox DevForum post, so it is platform ground truth rather than an indie self-report. It is a benchmark for roadmap planning, not a rule for individual solo projects.
- N7595 is dated 2026-09, so the 79% on-time figure reflects platform roadmap delivery as of late 2026 and is the most recent benchmark in this batch.
- Tool build-time figures (N7688, Oct 2026) compare Claude, Roblox Assistant and Lemonade on one self-reported test. These tools change quickly, so re-benchmark before relying on them.
- The 79% on-time shipping rate (N7650, Sep 2026) is official Roblox DevForum data about the platform roadmap, not a per-game benchmark.
- Mobile Studio typing speed (T17974, Oct 2026) may change as Studio improves on mobile.
- The ABD remake timeline (N7614) includes a later cancellation (ABDR), so use it as a cautionary example rather than a norm.
- The 2-month learning timeline (T17861, N7634) reflects one creator's experience from 2026 and is self-reported.
- Newest source is Roblox DevForum (N7848, 2026-09); Roblox's on-time figure is the most current platform data.
- SimTek (T18303, 2025-09 and T18356, 2026-04) is the most recent creator advice on Studio tooling and publishing.
- ByteBlox's AI comments (T18165, Q1852) date from 2025-01. AI tools have changed quickly since then, so the 'manual is faster' view may be outdated by November 2026. Re-test before relying on it.
- ItsZyonic's Studio Light advice (T18070, T18072) is from 2023-11. Mobile build features may have changed since then.
- Emancyphur's 2021 advice (T17996, T18009, T18017, T18018, N7739) is older. Tutorial-version and Studio-version advice may need checking against current Studio.
- Git advice (T18242, 2025-05) is recent and reflects current Studio undo limits.
- T18411 (2020-03) is the oldest item; the gamepass publish requirement should be re-checked against the current Creator Hub.
- T18436 (2022-03) and T18554 (2024-11) predate the 2025-2026 advice; treat as general principles rather than current platform facts.
- N8028 (2026-09, Roblox DevForum) is the most recent official figure and should override indie estimates on roadmap timing.
- Most new items are from 2024-2026; prefer them when they conflict with older items.
- T18724 (2026-10) and T18646 (2026-03) reflect current Studio and live-event practice; verify against current platform changes.
- N8034 is dated 2026-09 and reflects roadmap delivery since the last RDC. Treat it as a point-in-time platform figure; re-check it after the next RDC.
- N8170 is the newest benchmark (official Roblox DevForum, 2026-09) and should take priority for roadmap planning.
- rasdev items (2026-05) are the most recent process advice here; they cover packaging timing.
- janisjanis01 items are from 2024 to 2025 and reflect the workflow and deadlines of that era (Easter updates, icon counts); treat them as examples, not current benchmarks.
- Hours and days in N8048 do not match (120 hours in 5 days), so treat the figure with caution.
- Today is 2026-11-01; the sources predate about 1 to 2 years of platform change.
- N8208 (2026-09, official Roblox DevForum) gives a 79% on-time roadmap shipping rate. Use it to justify buffers in roadmap and deadline planning; it is a platform figure and does not measure indie release dates.

## Instructions for an agent using this file
- If the user is a beginner, then recommend a simple, finishable game (simulator, obby, hot potato) and scope to under ~1 month.
- If asked what to build first, then define a single core loop MVP and defer extra features to post-launch updates.
- If a mechanic is risky or physics-heavy, then recommend a narrow Studio prototype (1-2 weeks) before full development.
- If a game has no traction after 1-2 weeks, then suggest pivoting or shelving; if it is a live game with an audience, advise continuing updates.
- If planning a live update, then apply a 2-3 week cap, use existing systems, put it behind a config flag, and run a retro afterward.
- If giving schedule estimates, then use 6 productive hours per day and add buffer (solo ~2x).
- If a deadline looms, then cut features, fix critical bugs, and ship.
- If the user wants a long roadmap, then advise quarter-to-6-month planning (newer) over year-long plans.
- If quoting indie income, speed or revenue claims, then label them unverified self-reports; prefer official statements for platform mechanics.
- If using AI to build, then advise step-by-step prompts with playtests; if a team grows, then add task boards, per-discipline owners, drafts after ~10 engineers, Git, and backup scripters.
- If a user is planning a first game, set expectations that the first 3-4 games may flop; advise learning from each and continuing.
- If a game has under about 10 DAU, prioritise D1 retention and average playtime over monetisation spend, but keep monetisation designed in.
- If a user is scoping an MVP, recommend a bite-size build around one core loop targeting 5-6 weeks, then iterate.
- If community suggests new features, log them in the backlog and check them against the core loop and deadline before building.
- If a user plans badge creation, check the 5-free-per-24h GMT limit and batch badges; each extra costs 100 Robux.
- If config changes are planned, stage them in Studio and test with SetTestingValue before publishing to live.
- If new events are created, keep them private until promotion time; if content is flagged as too mature, modify it with PolicyService and resubmit.
- If an update changes any questionnaire answer, tell the user to re-take and resubmit it; after a memory-leak fix, verify with the next update's crash chart.
- If the user asks about AI-assisted scripting, present Roblox's demo and productivity claims as self-reported and advise verifying output in Studio.
- If Luau CI testing or Rojo is proposed, check Open Cloud testing availability first and prefer Studio file sync with VS Code.
- If a beginner asks how long a first game takes, answer with the 2-4 week MVP at 5-15 hours per week and say it is the newer figure.
- If a feature is estimated over 2-3 weeks, propose a cheap stand-in first.
- If the user starts a large or ambitious project, ask whether the team size and time budget can finish it, then offer a smaller first release.
- If the user mentions AI for scripts, recommend small prompts, testing after each, and review of output; for beginners, suggest building without AI first.
- If the user is planning a release, check the release checklist: RNG and lock verification, publish before inviting testers, public app irreversibility, and moderation time for marketplace items.
- If the user is stuck, suggest switching to a different system for a while, then returning.
- If the user asks about scope, count content items times variants before estimating art time.
- If the user wants fast progress, recommend a template place for reusable systems instead of rebuilding from scratch.
- Do not use classroom lesson timings from the 2026-10 Roblox education docs as commercial game development benchmarks.
- When sources conflict on planning versus shipping, favour a short planning phase followed by a fast MVP release, then iterate on feedback.
- If a solo beginner's scope exceeds 2-4 weeks of estimated work, cut features until it fits, then ship.
- If a build passes its time-box (1-3 weeks, max 4), cut the least-retention-critical scope rather than extending it.
- If a playtest is about to start, define one specific goal and a one-minute limit before inviting testers.
- If the user has been away from a project for more than 2 days, prompt them to resume and pick the next small step.
- If the user wants to use AI for code, keep one main session for integration and have them review the output.
- If the user is about to publish a live game or Team Create place, check the Early Access opt-in and the autosave/publish status first.
- If the user asks for speed-up advice, prioritise workflow (hotkeys, folders, copy-and-convert, templates) over premature code optimisation.
- If the user plans a trend or jam game, check the time budget first (4-9 days for simple games, 48 hours for jams) and keep scope to one core mechanic.
- If the user plans a monetised project, have them run projected Robux numbers before committing months of build time.
- If a project is stuck, have the user redo the last few steps, ask three peers, then escalate.
- If a user plans a big update, first design data structures and systems on paper, then estimate with a generous buffer. Expect slips of roughly 3x on updates.
- If a user has a working but declining game, recommend an overhaul before abandoning it, unless it is game-breaking, in which case replace it.
- If a user has a rough playtest, recommend a focused fix window (about 2 weeks) before wider launch or paid traffic.
- If a user asks about AI-generated scripts, recommend using AI for logic and systems but requiring them to read and understand the code before shipping.
- If a team uses Team Create, explain Collaborative Editing drafts, prompt commits and the Merge From Server conflict flow.
- If a user is stuck on a single system after several attempts, suggest outsourcing it or cutting it from scope.
- If a user lists a mechanic that hurts controls or tempo in playtests, recommend cutting it.
- Do not treat self-reported build times as benchmarks; present them as ranges and note that they are self-reported.
- If a Roblox API or feature is labelled alpha or beta, expect bugs and breaking changes. Verify its behaviour in Studio before building core systems on it.
- If planning a full-cycle project, include post-launch update time and budget at the planning stage, not after launch.
- If a prototype is working and a partner is available, consider handing it off for expansion, but only after the core loop has been shown to work.
- Treat single-source items (T17417, T17517) as anecdotal and self-reported; do not present them as general rules without corroboration.
- If starting a significant feature, first run an AI grilling session and log the answers as decisions before writing code.
- If an agent is about to build from a prompt, first have it write a plan and review that plan before building.
- If setting up auto-publish, store the Open Cloud key on disk and never paste it into chat or commit it to the repo.
- If a change needs to be tested, prefer committing to trigger auto-publish and verifying on a phone over waiting for a manual publish.
- If a feature has interactive objects, scope the functional parts (scripts, motors, axles) as well as the visual mesh.
- If you are reusing AI workflows across a team, put the skills in the project repository.
- If a live game's foundation can no longer support its scale, plan a foundation rebuild; reserve whole-game replacement for game-breaking problems.
- If you hit a fundamental problem in a live game, evaluate foundation rebuild before abandoning it.
- Do not use Compute functions in current plans; treat them as Early 2027 unless the platform announces otherwise.
- When using AI in design, use it to ask questions and surface gaps, and keep the decisions with the human.
- When advising on schedule risk for a Roblox roadmap or a team's delivery plan, cite the 79% on-time benchmark as a reference point and build buffer for roughly one in five items slipping.
- If a user is a beginner heading toward a first release, set expectations of about two months of practice (T17861) and suggest drilling each service in a scratch baseplate first (T17859).
- If a user is following a tutorial, tell them to keep Studio open and rebuild the system unaided afterward (T17858, Q1821).
- If a user plans a timed or jam build, limit scope to one mechanic and reserve publish time at the end (T17977, N7714).
- If a user is building on mobile Studio, add extra scripting time and warn that typing is slow (T17974, N7713).
- If a user asks which platform to launch on first, recommend a single platform for a first release (T17931), and note that the older cross-platform advice applies once they have experience.
- If a user is testing a loop with long timers, suggest a temporary test config to shorten them and restore before release (T17799).
- When citing build times, label them as self-reported creator figures (N7688, N7626), not benchmarks.
- When citing the 79% figure, attribute it to the official Roblox DevForum post and say it is platform-level (N7650).
- If a user asks about the placement or build-by-player feature, build the client snapping pass first, then add server validation before release.
- If a user asks about templates, store shared templates in ReplicatedStorage and per-player GUI in ServerStorage, cloned per player.
- If a user is building on a phone, scope to single-player and recommend a PC for scripting.
- If a user is prototyping a small game, drop multiplayer until the core loop is fun.
- If an AI-assisted fix loop fails repeatedly, stop prompting and write the code manually.
- If a user is planning roadmap dates, add a realistic miss rate (about 21% late per the official 79% on-time figure).
- If a user asks about project setup, recommend version control from day one rather than relying on Studio undo.
- If a user is duplicating a game into a new place, reuse the scripts and change only the place IDs.
- If a user is renaming a symbol in a script, use the Studio rename-symbol refactor rather than find and replace.
- Treat indie figures and tips as self-reported; treat N7848 as official ground truth.
- If the user asks to create gamepasses, first confirm the experience is published (T18411).
- If the user plans roadmap dates, pad them using the 79% on-time baseline (N8028) and plan for about one in five items to slip.
- If the user reports unbalanced progression, first confirm the core loop is fun, then consider adding content before tuning numbers (T18560).
- If the user is a beginner aiming for a full game, recommend scripting first with 2-3 hours a day of Studio practice (T18637, T18646).
- If the user is considering paid user acquisition or a funding raise, ask for traction evidence first (T18574).
- If the user wants to show a release is an improvement, compare retention, playtime and CCU against the previous release (T18554).
- When citing indie figures (CCU spikes, practice hours, backpack counts), label them as self-reported and single-case.
- If a user has less than one week to a deadline, pick the batch they can finish, drop the rest, and do not extend the deadline.
- If a user is polishing title, icon, or description on a game whose core loop is not yet fun, say so and recommend testing the core loop first.
- If a user is setting roadmap or release dates, use the official 79% on-time base rate and add buffer for slip.
- When citing janisjanis01 figures, label them as self-reported examples from 2024 to 2025, not benchmarks.
- When reviewing a mechanic, check each step in the core loop for friction that adds no fun, and suggest removing it.
- Prefer the rasdev 2026 guidance on packaging timing over older advice when they conflict.
- If a user plans spawns for a lobby-based game, recommend several transparent, non-collidable spawn locations spread around the lobby.
- If a user asks how much schedule slip to expect, cite the 79% on-time figure (N8208) as a platform reference and add buffer accordingly.

## Not covered here (research elsewhere)
- Concrete criteria for 'no traction' (CCU, retention thresholds) beyond anecdotes.
- Budgeting and costs for hiring contractors, and pay rates.
- Legal/contract details for team splits and revenue sharing.
- Detailed testing/QA processes and automated testing in Roblox.
- Handling team conflict, motivation and long-term burnout beyond brief mentions.
- Current (2026) Roblox policy on review, publishing and AI-generated content.
- Sufficiently verified benchmarks for D1/D7 retention targets guiding iteration.
- No D1/D7 retention benchmarks or playtime targets beyond the ~10 DAU threshold.
- No sprint length, team size or role-split guidance beyond daily or twice-weekly team meetings.
- No cost, revenue or return-on-effort figures to justify scope decisions.
- No quantified kill or pivot criteria (metrics that trigger scrapping a game).
- PolicyService specifics and how maturity ratings map to audience settings are not detailed.
- Open Cloud testing availability and the full Luau CI setup are not confirmed.
- No estimation method (story points, time-boxing) or buffer percentages beyond the existing file's rules.
- No detail on live-ops cadence costs or staffing for weekly updates.
- Feedback triage and retrospective process details are not covered here.
- No data on retention, conversion or monetisation rates beyond a single ad-benchmark rule (Yann, day 5).
- No post-launch growth benchmarks except Fisch (1B plays in about 2 months, self-reported).
- No guidance on marketing, discovery or thumbnails tied to development speed.
- No team-scaling data beyond the RoDev six-person example and the 99 Nights three-person example.
- Very few sources cover concrete code review processes or source control beyond single-creator anecdotes; no Roblox-specific version control workflow beyond Rokit and Rojo mentions.
- No sources on how many playtests or players are needed before a design decision is validated.
- Most figures are self-reported by indie creators and unverified; treat them as anecdotes.
- No cost data for paid assets, commissioned work or outsourced editing beyond a general statement about hiring an editor.
- Little on long-term live-ops cadence (weekly or monthly update rhythms) beyond 'ship frequent updates and let data drive them'.
- No retention, revenue or conversion benchmarks beyond D1/D30 beta guidance; no DAU-to-monetisation data.
- No evidence on how often trend or template games succeed relative to core-idea games; the claims are anecdotal.
- The sources conflict on whether to monetise early or hold monetisation until about 10 DAU; no tested data resolves it.
- Current status of Server Authority GA, Avatar Abilities and Character Controller Library after 2026-06 is not covered; verify on the DevForum or creator docs.
- No guidance on team size, hiring, or compensation for collaborators beyond one friend-first recruiting tip.
- Most figures are self-reported indie timelines, not controlled studies; treat them as ranges, not benchmarks.
- Roblox Jumpstart programme details, eligibility and current intake are only mentioned, not explained.
- No coverage of live-ops analytics tooling, A/B testing methods, or how to read crash charts in depth.
- No guidance on contracts, legal or tax aspects of publishing paid content.
- Mature-content and moderation timing for launch planning is only partly covered by existing rules; check the current moderation policy.
- No sources on monetisation timing or pricing beyond existing rules; check Roblox DevEx and developer documentation.
- No sources on hiring, contractor costs or team-size economics for outsourcing systems.
- No comparison of AI coding tools or prompt workflows; verify current tooling and its licensing.
- No quantified retention or D1/D30 benchmarks beyond existing rules; use Roblox analytics dashboards.
- No guidance on git or version control for Roblox projects beyond Team Create; check Roblox's Studio version history and external tooling.
- No evidence on post-launch marketing, ads or social promotion for these tactics; research separately.
- No data on how long to keep a rebuild of a declining game versus starting a new title; compare plays and revenue per project.
- No sources on destruction, welding and performance algorithms beyond one creator's note; consult Roblox engineering docs for physics and performance.
- Which specific Roblox public API is in alpha (Q1763), its expected timeline, and when it is expected to reach stable.
- Concrete cost or time figures for a post-launch update budget (T17517 gives no numbers).
- Terms of partner-studio expansion (ownership, revenue split, timeline) for T17417.
- Sample size and outcome data for the T17417 and T17517 claims; both are single-source and self-reported.
- Evidence on how much post-launch update spending affects retention or revenue.
- No specifics on what Scene Analysis checks, thresholds, or how to read its output.
- No detail on Compute functions limits, pricing, or migration path from game servers.
- No guidance on Open Cloud key scope, rotation, or security beyond keeping it on disk.
- Plan mode and agent skills lack concrete steps, tool names, or file formats in these sources.
- No indie validation of AI-assisted commit or auto-publish workflows; the only indie source (GamesBeat, T17602) is self-reported and covers Studio collaboration, not AI workflows.
- The on-time shipping rate (79%) and feature count (130) lack definitions and do not translate directly to indie solo or small-team benchmarks.
- No guidance on how to judge when a foundation is outgrown versus when it needs incremental refactoring.
- Safety and moderation of kids' AI-assisted commits (N7572) are not covered.
- The Q1791 quote is short and its exact context is unclear; treat it as motivational, not operational.
- The source does not define the RDC period or the sample size behind the 79% figure.
- No indie or solo-developer on-time rates were provided; this figure is platform-level only.
- The source does not say which roadmap items slipped or why.
- N7593 gives no sample size, time window detail beyond 'since last RDC', or definition of 'on time' beyond the summary, so verify before quoting it precisely.
- The source gives only the aggregate on-time rate; it does not say how 'on time' is defined beyond the stated window, what counts as a slip, or how indie developers' miss rates compare. Research these elsewhere before applying the figure to a solo or small-team schedule.
- No revenue, retention or player-count outcomes are linked to the new timing or platform advice; these are mostly self-reported process notes.
- Most new rules have only one creator source (strength single-mention or one-creator-repeated), so they need corroboration from other creators.
- The sources do not explain how the 79% on-time figure was measured or what counts as an on-time roadmap item.
- The AI tool comparison (N7688) lacks output quality, bug count or maintainability measures, so speed is the only comparison.
- No official Roblox guidance is given on beta labelling, beta duration or store-page expectations for beta releases.
- No guidance on mobile Studio workflow limits beyond one creator's timed build.
- The sources do not cover how long the timed-build challenges' rules or judging worked in detail.
- No data on what Studio Light (mobile) currently supports, such as limits on features or publishing from a phone.
- No data on Git workflow details, such as branching, commit cadence or how to combine Git with native file sync.
- No retention, DAU or revenue figures tied to development speed or release cadence in these new items.
- No detail on how the 79% on-time figure is calculated, or what the RDC roadmap covers.
- No information on how much time the 6-hour challenge actually produced for a solo beginner.
- No detail on Q1864 (Duckable) about the day spent on character arms, so the specific fix and time cost are unknown.
- No evidence on how much traction is enough before spending on UA or raising money.
- No benchmarks for gamepass conversion, pricing or revenue per player.
- The 79% on-time figure is official, but the sample period and item scope beyond 'since last RDC' are not detailed.
- The CCU 100 to 550 spike and the backpack count come from single creators with no sample size or control.
- No guidance on how to measure 'tangibly better' releases beyond naming the goal.
- The 60-day growth plan has no content or method behind it in the notes.
- The current gamepass creation flow in 2026 has not been verified against this source.
- N8034 gives only the aggregate 79% figure. It does not break down on-time rates by item type, team size, or slip length, so it cannot tell a solo developer how much buffer to add.
- No indie-creator equivalent of the on-time rate is covered by this item; the figure is official platform data, not self-reported indie data.
- No data on how batch size was chosen or how long each batch took by content type.
- No retention, revenue, or post-launch numbers linked to these process choices.
- Build-time figure in N8048 is unclear on hours per day; no clean hours-per-week benchmark.
- No detail on which RDC roadmap items make up the 79% figure, or how 'on time' is defined beyond 'since the last RDC'.
- No guidance on how to vet an open-source module for quality or licensing before adopting it.
- No specifics on the social montage format, platform, or posting cadence.
- Sprint length and team structure for rasdev's process are not stated.
- Sources do not say what N8208 counts as an item or how large the sample is, so treat the 79% figure with caution.
- No details on how spawn locations interact with teams, respawns or game modes beyond the lobby case (T18962).
- T18986 (store templates in ReplicatedStorage and clone at runtime) repeats the existing rule on shared templates and was not added as a new rule; no further detail on eggs-folder patterns is covered.
- No indie data on how often small Roblox projects hit their dates; research elsewhere before setting timelines for solo developers.

## Quotes (near-verbatim)
- "No game gets popular straight away, mine took over a year. Don't rush your builds, put time and effort into them." ([Roblox DevForum 2016-04 ](https://devforum.roblox.com/t/building-tutorial-for-all-resources-available/23919))
- "We are jogging, not sprinting!" ([Roblox DevForum 2017-11 ](https://devforum.roblox.com/t/game-design-theory-planning-a-game-and-implementing-features/62838))
- "Your first pass localization will pretty much be a weekend project, but getting it right can take a lot more effort." ([Roblox 2018-07 2:21](https://youtu.be/qoWMP-AFT08?t=141))
- "Polishing animations by playing them over and over is annoying, playtesting over and over to make small tweaks is maddening." ([Roblox DevForum 2020-05 ](https://devforum.roblox.com/t/procedural-animation-what-it-is-and-how-to-do-it/564782))
- "Lots of tedious task that would take 30 minutes turned into seconds." ([Roblox DevForum 2020-05 ](https://devforum.roblox.com/t/game-design-theory-project-management-with-devops-analytics-and-pipelines-for-successful-games-and-productive-teams/568057))
- "You should never be spending 1 to 3 months of resources on your team for something that you're going to ship and then just leave to die in your game." ([Roblox Creators 2021-07 5:16](https://youtu.be/UL4QaSbfyC4?t=316))
- "If you're looking to release the perfect feature, you're risking never shipping that feature because there's always something that you can do better on the feature." ([Roblox Creators 2021-07 10:40](https://youtu.be/HKLRLhHHHVc?t=640))
- "Ultimately your goal should be to release when ready, not just because players are demanding it." ([Roblox Creators 2021-07 26:04](https://youtu.be/HKLRLhHHHVc?t=1564))
- "If it's game breaking I must replace it; if it's not game breaking I keep it in as long as humanly possible." ([Last Level Studios 2021-08 6:33](https://youtu.be/3djSOjdGaE8?t=393))
- "For me as an economy designer, the only thing that matters is the amount of effort, a.k.a. time that goes into a player completing a feature." ([Roblox Creators 2021-09 43:01](https://youtu.be/k2gbQI-ggB4?t=2581))
- "Content cadence should not introduce new features, currencies or one off mechanics. Save those big undertakings for your game expansions." ([Roblox Creators 2021-10 0:29](https://youtu.be/d4g30UupgKw?t=29))
- "If people aren't playing your game from the get-go it's maybe time to move on." ([Roblox Creators 2021-11 28:44](https://youtu.be/rVr0IyfYkPg?t=1724))
- "Just do it... don't be so worried about making everything perfect." ([Roblox Creators 2021-11 41:52](https://youtu.be/rVr0IyfYkPg?t=2512))
- "Wireframes are probably your best friend in actually designing UI." ([Roblox Creators 2021-12 3:31](https://youtu.be/cY1B5_x4RoU?t=211))
- "The crunch game dev cycle is just not sustainable in live ops." ([Roblox Creators 2022-01 16:40](https://youtu.be/oV8DKuAjQQs?t=1000))
- "prototypes are critical for detecting design flaws and oversights early in development" ([Roblox Creators 2022-02 0:13](https://youtu.be/iI2t7Yd7Byc?t=13))
- "these are heavily under utilized by developers out there" ([Roblox Creators 2022-02 2:14](https://youtu.be/iI2t7Yd7Byc?t=134))
- "don't get caught up in the just one more of this just one more of that before i release this game" ([Roblox Creators 2022-05 1:04:45](https://youtu.be/xBlHz30PMKo?t=3885))
- "Prototype is the first milestone you want to hit and MVP is the last one you want to hit before you eventually ship your game." ([Roblox Creators 2022-06 35:13](https://youtu.be/Mep4_w8qxeE?t=2113))
- "For this reason, do not aim for perfection in a game, aim for completion." ([Roblox DevForum 2022-08 ](https://devforum.roblox.com/t/the-art-of-robux-getting/1922578))

- "Our current systems were not able to handle the things that we want. We need to rewrite most of our scripts and the entire item system because the whole game was built around items." ([Lnor Roblox 2025-12 3:59](https://youtu.be/Nywo2NwQPNo?t=239))
- "Roblox as a platform moves very quickly. So you have to be fast with the creation of games and the release of games." ([Daiorg 2025-12 44:05](https://youtu.be/JCMIb008Y6E?t=2645))
- "You can still make something cool with like the limitations." ([Creators on the Blox 2025-12 1:30:07](https://youtu.be/LyJ04iE6K1M?t=5407))
- "This idea lasted 3 days before I decided I wanted to add Parkour!" ([DitchyDevelopments 2026-03 0:00](https://youtu.be/O9fIqTik9ik?t=0))
- "make sure that your game is fun before you start adding stuff to it." ([Creators on the Blox 2026-03 48:11](https://youtu.be/VyBSvWSIkao?t=2891))
- "we struggled to update it cuz the scripting in the game was hard" ([1_Bruce 2026-03 1:16](https://youtu.be/zgq6L4ZzCHs?t=76))
- "Have the framework and the groundwork for it come first and then build all your details on top." ([Creators on the Blox 2026-03 1:04:56](https://youtu.be/LWaw0KNwxyM?t=3896))
- "At your stage in development, speed is way more important than polish and no bugs." ([rasdev 2026-05 3:18](https://youtu.be/q0lIbkUC2r8?t=198))
- "It's not this linear process, it's this curved road. There's forks in it all over." ([Creators on the Blox 2026-05 16:01](https://youtu.be/JsiEVtWLwnA?t=961))
- "When you give that release date, it's based off what you have at that point in time; things come up you couldn't have calculated for." ([Creators on the Blox 2026-05 17:23](https://youtu.be/JsiEVtWLwnA?t=1043))
- "Premature optimization is the root of evil. ... you should not be obsessed over efficiency." ([Roblox DevForum 2026-05 ](https://devforum.roblox.com/t/the-definitive-tutorial-to-game-optimization-everything-you-need-to-know/4629258))
- "Once you have your game design document, this is kind of like the scope of the project." ([Tizzy 2026-05 11:38](https://youtu.be/7tYPvXYlif8?t=698))
- "Just launch and see how players respond, because the quicker you can get live players in there, the quicker you can get data." ([Tizzy 2026-05 14:23](https://youtu.be/7tYPvXYlif8?t=863))
- "I spent months on a project that I ended up scrapping. So, I wanted to make something really, really quick." ([Roblox 2026-05 0:44](https://youtu.be/-14vhiLL4xE?t=44))
- "Simple games usually are the best." ([rasdev 2026-06 4:05](https://youtu.be/q0uePCbBlk0?t=245))
- "Do it every single day and you will get better. There is no getting worse at it." ([Creators on the Blox 2026-06 1:13:19](https://youtu.be/5vJCBrxPHmY?t=4399))
- "A tiny finished game with 12 players is worth more than a massive unfinished game with zero." ([ArTheDev 2026-06 2:43](https://youtu.be/wG9XzLnJC5k?t=163))
- "He updated daily, sometimes twice a day, and he followed the data." ([Tizzy 2026-06 0:42](https://youtu.be/HFcegpPPvpI?t=42))
- "99% of developers think they can get something done quicker than they truly can." ([Yann 2026-06 2:03](https://youtu.be/zWeDpmrjhUM?t=123))
- "Don't ever just try to wait for the perfect moment or make the perfect game." ([rasdev 2026-07 4:06](https://youtu.be/fPT2GCkK1QM?t=246))
- "Fable 5 is getting to the point where that's basically not a problem anymore. You still need to understand game design." ([MoseyTube 2026-07 4:07](https://youtu.be/1Oicl2fFt3o?t=247))
